    Important Words
    • bhāskaraḥ — the sun
      Kṛṣṇa is repeatedly compared to the sun in the SB (1.3.43 — sun of dharma; 10.14.36 — sun of devotee-protection). The simile here is liturgical: His daily rising is the inner truth behind the cosmic sunrise.
    • sātyaki-uddhava — Sātyaki and Uddhava
      Two paradigmatic Vaiṣṇavas: Sātyaki the warrior-bhakta, Uddhava the contemplative-bhakta. Together they show that bhakti has both fierce-active and inward-reflective faces, both at Kṛṣṇa's side.
    • pūrva-adrim — the eastern mountain
      Udayācala, the mythic mountain over which the sun rises each day. Kṛṣṇa's chariot is His personal udayācala — wherever He mounts becomes the eastern horizon for the world.
    • pāṇinā pāṇī — hand to hands
      Bhagavān takes the charioteer's hands in His own as He mounts — physical touch initiated by the Lord. Such touch is what the gopīs called śrī-aṅga-saṅga, the supreme grace, given here to a servant who only drives.
    Holding on to His charioteer’s hands, Lord Kṛṣṇa would mount the chariot, together with Sātyaki and Uddhava, just like the sun rising over the easternmost mountain.
    Purport

    The ācāryas point out that the Lord’s chariot driver would stand with joined palms and that the Lord, holding on to his joined hands with His right hand, would mount the chariot.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Holding on to His charioteer's hands, Lord Kṛṣṇa would mount the chariot, together with Sātyaki and Uddhava, just like the sun rising over the easternmost mountain.
    Commentary

    Taking the folded hands of his charioteer with his right hand he would mount the chariot.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The chariot was huge like a mountain. The Lord was shining like the sun because of his ornaments. The example indicates the magnificence of the chariot.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Atha indicates auspiciousness. Sātyaki and Uddhava were the dearest servants, and thus stood by his side holding umbrella and cāmara. The chariot was huge like a mountain. The Lord was shining like the sun because of his ornaments. The example indicates the magnificence of the chariot.

    Related values & verses
    Concepts
    Jiva
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Kṛṣṇa mounting His chariot with Sātyaki and Uddhava is the sun rising over the eastern mountain — except the sun has chosen His own two horizon-peaks to rise between. Bhakti makes the cosmos personal: even His mounting has named companions, not anonymous space.

    Stories
    Uddhava as Kṛṣṇa's other self

    In SB 11.6 Kṛṣṇa tells Uddhava, 'You are non-different from Me' (tvaṁ tu sākṣād bhagavān). Uddhava is His second body, His confidant, the one to whom He will entrust the Uddhava-gītā at the end of His earth-līlā.

    In this verse, decades earlier, Uddhava is already standing in the chariot beside Him. Sakhya-rasa is not built in a day — it is years of standing beside Him on every morning's ride.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.70.16

    ↗
    ఈక్షితోఽన్త:పురస్‍త్రీణాం సవ్రీడప్రేమవీక్షితై: । కృచ్ఛ్రాద్ విసృష్టో నిరగాజ్జాతహాసో హరన్ మన: ॥ ౧౬ ॥
    īkṣito ’ntaḥ-pura-strīṇāṁ

    Important Words
    • sa-vrīḍa-prema — shy and loving
      Vrīḍa (shyness) plus prema (love) — the technical pair the Gauḍīyas analyse as the gopīs' signature mood. The queens of Dvārakā partake in mahiṣī-bhāva, which in Gauḍīya theology is itself a refraction of Vraja mādhurya.
    • kṛcchrāt visṛṣṭaḥ — released with difficulty
      Even Bhagavān finds it hard to leave them — His own form is held by the bhāva of His devotees. This is the doctrine of bhakta-vatsala: Kṛṣṇa is captive to His devotees' love, not its master.
    • jāta-hāsaḥ — a smile having arisen
      The smile of departure — Kṛṣṇa-smita — is the Gauḍīya signature image. From it Rūpa Gosvāmī begins his rasa-analysis: nothing wounds the devotee more, nothing sustains them more, than that smile.
    • haran manaḥ — stealing the mind
      Manohara — mind-stealer — is one of Kṛṣṇa's epithets. He is the original thief: of butter in Vraja, of vastra at Cīraghāṭa, and here of the queens' minds. Bhakti is the consent of the stolen one.
    The palace women would look upon Lord Kṛṣṇa with shy, loving glances, and thus He would get free from them only with difficulty. He would then set off, His smiling face captivating their minds.
    Purport

    Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī describes this scene as follows: “The shy, loving glances of the palace women, hinting at their agitation, implied, ‘How can we tolerate this torment of being separated from You?’ The idea here is that because the Lord was captured by their affection, He smiled, indicating ‘My dear restless ladies, you are so overwhelmed by this little bit of separation. I am coming back later today to enjoy with you.’ And then, with His smile captivating their minds, He got away only with difficulty, freeing Himself from the bondage of their loving glances.”

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    The palace women would look upon Lord Kṛṣṇa with shy, loving glances, and thus He would get free from them only with difficulty. He would then set off, His smiling face captivating their minds.
    Commentary

    First the queens glanced at Krsna, though he was accompanied by Satyaki, Uddhava and others, with shy loving glances. This indicated their pain of separation from him "How can we endure this?" By this hint of perplexity, he became bound. He smiled to pacify them. "O unsteady women, just by this little separation you are disturbed. I will return very soon to eat." Then bewildering their minds with just a smile, he got out of difficulty: freed from the bondage of their loving glances (krcchrat visrstah) he went off.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    His verse shows the situation before he mounted the chariot. This verse describes the prema of the women who were holding articles for the auspiciousness of the journey inside and outside the palace, for Kṛṣṇa, the object of auspiciousness in performing daily duties, who came out alone, after all the people who had come from outside the city had left. The palace women glanced at him with prema, trying to stop him from leaving with their glances. Then by slightly lowering their glances they would allow him to depart (visṛsṭaḥ). After that he would smile, signifying, “Oh! O woman your intelligence has been covered by prema. Why do you tremble? I will come for a meal at noon.” He desired to pacify them with his smile.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    A smile would manifest because his heart was always pleased. Or he produced another rasa by smiling to lighten the pain of separation. Thus he attracted their minds. He would free himself with difficult and depart or depart with auspicious calls from others such as “Be victorious!” (visṛṣṭaḥ).

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Sthitaprajna (Steady Wisdom)Vairagya (Detachment / Dispassion)Discriminative WisdomAbhaya (Fearlessness)Seva (Service / Selfless Action)Samata (Equanimity / Even-mindedness)Ahimsa (Non-violence / Compassion)Anahankarah (Freedom from False Ego)
    Related verses
    BG 5.7 BG 12.5 BG 5.4 BG 16.2 BG 9.26 BG 3.27 BG 10.4 BG 3.11 BG 18.1 BG 2.55 BG 7.8
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    The queens' shy glances at the departing Kṛṣṇa are the gopīs' parting glances at the rāsa transposed to Dvārakā — like a flame catching the corner of a curtain as the door closes. The smile He turns back over His shoulder is the same smile that left Vṛndāvana once and never stopped haunting it.

    Stories
    The gopīs at Akrūra's chariot

    In SB 10.39, when Akrūra's chariot bore Kṛṣṇa away from Vṛndāvana, the gopīs ran behind it weeping, holding it back with their glances when their hands could not. Kṛṣṇa's last smile to them entered the heart of the Gauḍīya tradition as the seed of all vipralambha-bhakti.

    Here at Dvārakā the wives play the same role each morning. Every separation rehearses that first separation; every smile of His remembers it.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.70.17

    ↗
    సుధర్మాఖ్యాం సభాం సర్వైర్వృష్ణిభి: పరివారిత: । ప్రావిశద్ యన్నివిష్టానాం న సన్త్యఙ్గ షడూర్మయ: ॥ ౧౭ ॥

    Important Words
    • sudharmā-ākhyām — known as Sudharmā
      Sudharmā literally 'excellent dharma' — the hall is named for the dharma it embodies. In a Gauḍīya frame, sudharma is bhāgavata-dharma, devotional service; nothing else qualifies.
    • ṣaṭ ūrmayaḥ — the six waves (of material life)
      The six waves are traditionally hunger, thirst, lamentation, illusion, old age, death — or per Śrīdhara, the dvandvas of the body, mind, and life. Bhagavān's hall protects those inside from all six. The image: His sannidhi is itself the antidote to saṁsāra.
    • vṛṣṇibhiḥ — by the Vṛṣṇis
      The Vṛṣṇi dynasty are Bhagavān's personal kinsmen, members of His eternal parivāra. The Gauḍīyas identify them as nitya-pārṣadas — eternal associates — not historical contingencies.
    • aṅga — my dear (King)
      Śuka addresses Parīkṣit with the intimate aṅga, the Bhāgavata's vocative of tenderness. The narration of Bhagavān's daily routine is itself offered as a refuge for the dying king.
    The Lord, attended by all the Vṛṣṇis, would enter the Sudharmā assembly hall, which protects those who enter it from the six waves of material life, dear King.
    Purport

    Śrīla Prabhupāda writes: “It may be remembered that the Sudharmā assembly house was taken away from the heavenly planet and was reestablished in the city of Dvārakā. The specific significance of the assembly house was that anyone who entered it would be free from the six kinds of material pangs, namely hunger, thirst, lamentation, illusion, old age and death. These are the waves of material existence, and as long as one remained in that assembly house of Sudharmā, he would not be affected by these six material waves.” In this regard, Śrīdhara Svāmī and Viśvanātha Cakravartī explain that when Lord Kṛṣṇa would exit separately from each of His many palaces, each individual form would be visible to the persons present on those particular palace grounds and to the neighboring residents, but not to others. Then, at the gateway path of the Sudharmā assembly hall, all the forms of the Lord would merge into a single form, and thus He would enter the hall.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    The Lord, attended by all the Vṛṣṇis, would enter the Sudharmā assembly hall, which protects those who enter it from the six waves of material life, dear King.
    Commentary

    Leaving each palace in each of his forms, each form would been seen only by the residents and surround neighbors of that particular palace, and not by others. Coming along the main road up to the entrance of the assembly hall, those forms would then become one, and he would enter the hall with only one form, surrounded (parivaritah) by the Vrsnis.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    No one would enter the assembly hall if Kṛṣṇa did not enter. He entered with music and songs (pra—aviśat). Though the hall was great from having been situated in Svarga, it was even greater because it was now in Kṛṣṇa’s city. Thus those who entered were not affected by material suffering.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    No one would enter the assembly hall if Kṛṣṇa did not enter. He entered with music and songs (pra—aviśat). Its attractive nature is shown. The six waves are lamentation, illusion, old age death, hunger and thirst. O king (aṅga)!

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Adambhitva (Non-hypocrisy / Authenticity)Vairagya (Detachment / Dispassion)Tejas (Inner Radiance / Vigour)Discriminative WisdomShanti (Peace / Tranquility)
    Related verses
    BG 5.18 BG 8.4 BG 14.12 BG 14.1 BG 12.1 BG 18.37 BG 11.1 BG 3.3 SB 1.2BG 12.3 BG 4.8 BG 15.9
    Concepts
    Jiva
    Related lectures
    • Radha Raman Sw SB 10-70-17-21 - 2015-07-22 ISKCON Alachua
      His Holiness Radha Raman Swami· 2015-07-22· English
    • VarsanaSw SB 10-70-17 - 2012-07-17 New Vrindavan
      His Holiness Varsana Swami· 2012-07-17· English
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    The Sudharmā hall — once Indra's, now Kṛṣṇa's — protects everyone inside from the six waves of saṁsāra. It is like a deity-room scaled up to a kingdom: entering is itself salvation, and the entrance-fee is only His company.

    Stories
    Sudharmā brought down from heaven

    In SB 10.50, after the defeat of Jarāsandha's allies, Kṛṣṇa brought the Sudharmā assembly hall down from Indra's heaven to Dvārakā. The same hall that once heard Indra's deliberations now seats Kṛṣṇa's.

    Gauḍīya commentators read this as Bhagavān relocating the centre of cosmic dharma: wherever He sits, that becomes the real Sudharmā, and the celestial original is only a shadow of His earthly court.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    the moon
    divi
    in the sky
    tārakā - gaṇaiḥ
    (surrounded) by the stars .
    Important Words
    • uḍu-rājaḥ — the king of the stars (the moon)
      The moon-among-stars simile is the SB's most beloved image for Bhagavān among His devotees (cf. 10.32.14, the gopīs in rāsa). Here it is applied to Dvārakā: every assembly of Vaiṣṇavas has Kṛṣṇa as its moon.
    • nr-siṁhaiḥ — by lions among men
      Nara-siṁha here is metaphorical for the Yadu heroes, but evocative of the Lord's own Nṛsiṁha-avatāra. Kṛṣṇa surrounded by 'lions among men' — His protectors are themselves portions of His own ferocity.
    • yadu-uttamaḥ — the best of the Yadus
      Kṛṣṇa is yadu-uttama, the supreme Yadu, even though He is svayam bhagavān. He chooses to take rank within His dynasty rather than apart from it — līlā always honours family.
    • sva-bhāsā — by His own effulgence
      His radiance is intrinsic, not borrowed. The same sva- that returned in the brāhma-muhūrta verse (sva-saṁsthayā, sva-śaktibhiḥ) marks Bhagavān as ever self-sufficient — and yet ever choosing relation.
    As the almighty Supreme Lord would seat Himself upon His exalted throne there in the assembly hall, He shone with His unique effulgence, illuminating all the quarters of space. Surrounded by the Yadus, lions among men, that best of the Yadus appeared like the moon amidst many stars.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    As the almighty Supreme Lord would seat Himself upon His exalted throne there in the assembly hall, He shone with His unique effulgence, illuminating all the quarters of space. Surrounded by the Yadus, lions among men, that best of the Yadus appeared like the moon amidst many stars.
    Commentary

    The best of the Yadus was surrounded by the best among men (nrsimhaih), the Yadus.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    There sitting on the most excellent throne, he shone because of his most excellent form, among his people who had been given similar forms. His throne also shone as the best among all the thrones including those of Ugrasena and others, even though these had been made most excellent. And he, as the supreme (vibhuḥ), shone in the Sudharmā hall, the best hall, endowed with the qualities of all best halls. He shone by his own effulgence, and illuminated all directions completely. He shone surrounded by the Yādavas since they, as his associates, were the best of all men, and since he was the best of the Yadus, displaying his special powers among the best of similar people. Thus the example of the moon and stars is apt.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    There, sitting on the most excellent throne, the Lord, full of all auspicious qualities (vibhuḥ), shone because of his beauty (bhāsā), but was especially beautiful among his people, because they were the very best, with special bhakti (nṛsīmhaiḥ). Or they were similar to Nṛsiṁhadeva, having great courage and possessiveness. The Lord was especially beautiful in the company of his devotees since he is affectionate to his devotees. The reasons is that the Lord appeared with excellence in the Yadu dynasty to manifest unlimited qualities like affection for his devotees (yadūttamaḥ). By his nature he manifested all beauty since he was the best of Yadus. An example is given to illustrate how the great beauty manifested in the special place, with his devotees. The moon is beautiful in the sky with the stars.

    Related values & verses

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Kṛṣṇa amidst the Yadu warriors is the moon among stars — but unlike the moon, He does not borrow His light. He gives light to every Yadu around Him, and yet they remain stars, not reflections. Acintya-bhedābheda: distinct yet of one effulgence.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.70.19

    ↗
    తత్రోపమన్త్రిణో రాజన్ నానాహాస్యరసైర్విభుమ్ । ఉపతస్థుర్నటాచార్యా నర్తక్యస్తాణ్డవై: పృథక్ ॥ ౧౯ ॥

    Important Words
    • upamantriṇaḥ — jesters, advisors-in-jest
      The court vidūṣaka — comic counselor — is a Vedic institution. Even in Bhagavān's assembly, laughter has its place. Bhakti is not solemn refusal of joy but joy redirected to Him.
    • hāsya-rasaiḥ — with comic moods, hāsya-rasas
      Hāsya is one of the nine rasas; even in the rasa-śāstra of bhakti, hāsya-rasa has its place (Bharata-muni, Rūpa Gosvāmī's Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu). Kṛṣṇa enjoys hāsya as ardently as vīra or mādhurya.
    • naṭa-ācāryāḥ — expert performers, dance-masters
      Naṭa-ācārya literally 'master-actor.' In Caitanya tradition the term is reclaimed: Mahāprabhu Himself is the supreme naṭa, dancing kṛṣṇa-līlā back into the world.
    • vibhum — the all-pervading Lord
      He is Vibhu — vast, all-pervading — and yet sits and receives the dance of one female performer. The vibhu is simultaneously aṇu in attention. Bhakti rests on this double infinity.
    And there, O King, jesters would entertain the Lord by displaying various comic moods, expert entertainers would perform for Him, and female dancers would dance energetically.
    Purport

    Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī points out that the word naṭācāryāḥ refers, among other things, to expert magicians. All of these different entertainers, one after the other, would perform for the Lord in the assembly of great kings.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    And there, O King, jesters would entertain the Lord by displaying various comic moods, expert entertainers would perform for Him, and female dancers would dance energetically.
    Commentary

    Jesters (upamantrinah), dancers (natacaryah) and magicians and others honored the Lord in their groups (prthak).

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Jesters entertained the Lord, full of all rasas (vibhum). Śukadeva addresses Parīkṣit in joy, “O king!” The performers served him by dancing.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Jesters entertained the Lord, full of all rasas (vibhum). Śukadeva addresses Parīkṣit in joy, “O king!” Or the performers shining with various rasas (rājan) served him by dancing.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Bhakti (Devotion)Prema (Divine Love)
    Concepts
    RasaMantraJiva
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Jesters and dancers in Bhagavān's court are like crows feeding from the same lake as swans — even play and laughter, in His presence, become bhakti. There is no domain of human life He has not made into His own service.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.70.20

    Important Words
    • mṛdaṅga — two-headed clay drum
      The mṛdaṅga is the Gauḍīya saṅkīrtana instrument par excellence; Mahāprabhu's parties were known by its sound. Its appearance here in Bhagavān's morning court establishes it as the eternal kīrtana-instrument.
    • veṇu — flute
      Veṇu in any Bhāgavata verse cannot be heard without recalling Kṛṣṇa's own flute at Vraja. Here in Dvārakā He has put down His flute and is being played for — the Vraja-instrument has become His courtly companion.
    • tuṣṭuvuḥ — they praised, offered stuti
      Stuti — formal verses of praise — is one of the nine limbs of bhakti (śravaṇa, kīrtana, smaraṇa, pāda-sevana, arcana, vandana, dāsya, sakhya, ātma-nivedana). The royal bards perform vandana daily.
    • māgadha — reciter of itihāsa, royal historian
      The māgadha recounts the king's ancestry. For Kṛṣṇa, this means reciting the līlās of His own previous avatāras and ancestors — itihāsa as theology. The Bhāgavatam itself is the perfected māgadha-recital.
    These performers would dance and sing to the sounds of mṛdaṅgas, vīṇās, murajas, flutes, cymbals and conchshells, while professional poets, chroniclers and panegyrists would recite the Lord’s glories.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    These performers would dance and sing to the sounds of mṛdaṅgas, vīṇās, murajas, flutes, cymbals and conchshells, while professional poets, chroniclers and panegyrists would recite the Lord's glories.
    Commentary

    Groups danced and other groups sang. The bards and others offered praises.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The singing and dancing of the bards was more exceptional than the previous singing and dancing.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The description is further enhanced. They sang and danced with drums etc. Or the chief performers (natācāryāḥ) would serve by dramatic gestures (tāṇḍavaih) and the female dancers would dance and sing with instrumental accompaniment. Muraja is a type of drum.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Bhakti (Devotion)Prema (Divine Love)
    Concepts
    RasaJiva
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Drums, vīṇās, flutes, cymbals, and conches — the whole orchestra of creation — playing in His court, with bards reciting His own glories: it is like the sea reciting itself back to the moon that pulls it. Bhakti makes the kīrtana of Kṛṣṇa the centre of every gathering, even His own.

    Stories
    Saṅkīrtana at Śrīvāsa-aṅgana

    In Caitanya-caritāmṛta, the saṅkīrtana parties at Śrīvāsa's house in Navadvīpa used the same instruments — mṛdaṅga, kartāla, vīṇā, śaṅkha — that this verse names in Dvārakā.

    The Six Gosvāmīs codified saṅkīrtana with these same instruments. The Dvārakā morning court here is the source-image: kīrtana of Kṛṣṇa in Kṛṣṇa's own presence is the eternal rasa, repeated wherever vaiṣṇavas gather.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.70.21

    Important Words
    • brahma-vādinaḥ — fluent in the Vedas
      Brahma-vādin is the technical term for the master of Vedic recitation. That such masters sit in Kṛṣṇa's court chanting establishes the Vedas themselves as His servants, not His sources.
    • puṇya-yaśasām — of pious fame
      Yaśas — fame — in śāstra is reckoned only when pious (puṇya). Material celebrity is not yaśas. The brāhmaṇas recite only those whose fame stands in dharma; Kṛṣṇa is Himself the source of all puṇya-yaśas.
    • kathāḥ — stories, narratives
      Kṛṣṇa-kathā and bhāgavata-kathā are the Gauḍīya tradition's central practice. Hearing kathā in the presence of Bhagavān is the SB's image of Vaikuṇṭha; the morning court is its earthly version.
    • āsīnāḥ — seated
      Sitting (āsīna) in Bhagavān's presence is itself a form of seva. The Six Gosvāmīs would sit before the deity reading kathā for hours. The posture is not passive — it is one of the most active possible.
    Some brāhmaṇas sitting in that assembly hall would fluently chant Vedic mantras, while others recounted stories of past kings of pious renown.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Some brāhmaṇas sitting in that assembly hall would fluently chant Vedic mantras, while others recounted stories of past kings of pious renown.
    Commentary

    Brahmanas chanted the Vedas (brahma ahuh) and those expert at speaking (vadinah) related stories of pious kings.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Brāhmaṇas who knew the meaning of the Vedas, chanted the Vedas. Other persons recited stories from the itihāsas and Purāṇas.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Brāhmaṇas who knew the meaning of the Vedas, or discussed the Vedas, chanted the Vedas. Other persons recited stories from the itihāsas and Purāṇas.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Adambhitva (Non-hypocrisy / Authenticity)Vairagya (Detachment / Dispassion)Tejas (Inner Radiance / Vigour)Discriminative Wisdom
    Related verses
    BG 5.18 BG 8.4 BG 14.12 BG 14.1 BG 12.1 BG 18.37 BG 11.1 BG 3.3 SB 1.2BG 12.3 BG 4.8 BG 15.9 BG 6.35 BG 18.74
    Concepts
    Jiva
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Brāhmaṇas chanting Vedic mantras and recounting tales of pious kings of old in Kṛṣṇa's court is like ancestors visiting their own descendant — the One whom every Veda sings and every pious king worshipped now sits and listens to His own praise.

    Stories
    Bhāgavata's vyāsa-pīṭha at the Naimiṣa forest

    The whole Bhāgavatam frame — sages at Naimiṣāraṇya hearing of pious kings from Sūta Gosvāmī — repeats the choreography of this verse. Kṛṣṇa's own court is the cosmic original of every śāstric assembly.

    The Gauḍīyas read every Bhāgavata-kathā session as a re-entry into this Dvārakā court: wherever Bhagavān is praised in the mouth of brāhmaṇas, the Sudharmā hall is locally re-established.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.70.22

    ↗
    తత్రైక: పురుషో రాజన్నాగతోఽపూర్వదర్శన: । విజ్ఞాపితో భగవతే ప్రతీహారై: ప్రవేశిత: ॥ ౨౨ ॥
    tatraikaḥ puruṣo rājann
    [tatraikah purusho raajann]

    tatra — there ekaḥ — one puruṣaḥ — person rājan — O King (Parīkṣit) āgataḥ — did come apūrva — never before darśanaḥ — whose appearance vijñāpitaḥ — announced bhagavate — to the Supreme Lord pratīhāraiḥ — by the doorkeepers praveśitaḥ — made to enter .

    tatra

    pratīhāraiḥ
    by the doorkeepers
    praveśitaḥ
    made to enter .
    Important Words
    • puruṣaḥ — a person, man
      Puruṣa is the Bhāgavata's resonant term — Bhagavān Himself is the puruṣottama, supreme puruṣa. Here a small puruṣa enters the court of the supreme puruṣa, a meeting that becomes the hinge of the chapter.
    • apūrva-darśanaḥ — whose appearance was unprecedented
      Never before seen at Dvārakā — the messenger represents lands and kings outside Kṛṣṇa's daily orbit. The verse signals that bhakti reaches beyond the inner circle: even strangers are heard.
    • bhagavate — to the Supreme Lord
      The doorkeepers announce him 'to Bhagavān' — not 'to the king.' The whole Bhāgavata vocabulary refuses to forget who Kṛṣṇa is, even in royal scenes.
    • praveśitaḥ — caused to enter
      The passive: he is brought in. Even arriving at Bhagavān is by His grace through His servants. No one enters Sudharmā by self-effort alone; the doorkeepers (His associates) escort the supplicant in.
    Once a certain person arrived in the assembly, O King, who had never been seen there before. The doorkeepers announced him to the Lord and then escorted him inside.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Once a certain person arrived in the assembly, O King, who had never been seen there before. The doorkeepers announced him to the Lord and then escorted him inside.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    A person they had never seen before (apūrva-darśanaḥ) arrived. The door keepers announced him and brought him in.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    A description of Kṛṣna’s typical forenoon activities has been given. One day, at that time, a king’s messenger arrived. An incident is related out of similarity to previous events described. The person had not been seen previously. O king! Śukadeva calls to the king, out of special rasa, concerning a different incident. He existed (rājan) as the messenger sent by the kings. Or he did not shine, had pale complexion (arājan), because of the great suffering of his masters. It was suitable that he was announced (vijṣāpitaḥ) to the Lord by the door keepers, because the Lord was revealing all his powers. Or he was announced to the Lord even though the Lord was omniscient (bhagavati). Or later by the Lord’s order (vijṣāpitaḥ), he was made to enter.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Bhakti (Devotion)Prema (Divine Love)

    • Sesa Pr SB 10-70-22-26 - 2015-07-23 ISKCON Alachua
      His Grace Sesa Prabhu· 2015-07-23· English
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    An unfamiliar messenger arrives at Bhagavān's court — and the doorkeepers, knowing nothing else of him, know enough to announce him to the Lord. Like a leaf borne on a river straight to the sea: every soul carrying a cry of suffering finds itself, without quite knowing how, at Kṛṣṇa's feet.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.70.23

    ↗
    స నమస్కృత్య కృష్ణాయ పరేశాయ కృతాఞ్జలి: । రాజ్ఞామావేదయద్ దు:ఖం జరాసన్ధనిరోధజమ్ ॥ ౨౩ ॥

    Important Words
    • kṛta-añjaliḥ — with joined palms
      Añjali — palms joined — is the universal Vaiṣṇava posture of supplication. Even a messenger does not address Bhagavān without this gesture. The body says what the words will say.
    • para-īśāya — to the Supreme Controller
      The messenger does not address Kṛṣṇa as merely a king but as parameśvara — Supreme Lord. The political appeal is folded inside a theological appeal: it is to Bhagavān, not to a king, that the imprisoned kings cry.
    • duḥkham — the suffering
      Bhagavān receives a report of duḥkha. The whole BG begins similarly with Arjuna reporting his duḥkha (BG 1.46-47). Bhakti often starts where pain finds words at Bhagavān's feet.
    • nirodha-jam — born of imprisonment
      Nirodha — confinement, blockage — is also the technical term in Bhāgavata cosmology for cosmic dissolution (SB 2.10.1). The imprisonment of the kings is a local nirodha within saṁsāra; only Bhagavān undoes both.
    That person bowed down to Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and with joined palms he described to the Lord how a number of kings were suffering because Jarāsandha had imprisoned them.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    That person bowed down to Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and with joined palms he described to the Lord how a number of kings were suffering because Jarāsandha had imprisoned them.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The messenger saw Kṛṣṇa as the Supreme Lord. He described the suffering of kings to Kṛṣṇa after offering respects. Or he spoke with sorrowful words about the kings.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The person bowed to Kṛṣna, who gives all happiness because he was directly the supreme Lord. Or he bowed to svayam bhagavān, who was endowed with all powers (pareśāya). Thus he informed the Lord of the suffering of the kings, as a messenger who had been given a letter by the kings secretly.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    The messenger bowing with joined palms and reporting the kings' suffering is like a single arrow drawn at the foot of the lion. He carries no weapon, only the news of pain — and that, before Bhagavān, is enough to set the rescue in motion.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.70.24

    ↗
    యే చ దిగ్విజయే తస్య సన్నతిం న యయుర్నృపా: । ప్రసహ్య రుద్ధాస్తేనాసన్నయుతే ద్వే గిరివ్రజే ॥ ౨౪ ॥

    Important Words
    • sannatim — complete subservience
      Saṁ-nati — full bowing, total submission. The kings refused to bow to Jarāsandha and were imprisoned for it. The Gauḍīya reading: refusal to bow to any but Bhagavān may bring worldly imprisonment, but it is the only correct refusal.
    • giri-vraje — in the fortress (city) of Girivraja
      Girivraja, Jarāsandha's capital in Magadha (modern Rajgir), is the geography of bondage in this chapter. Kṛṣṇa will eventually enter it with Bhīma and Arjuna (SB 10.71-72) to undo it from inside.
    • ayute dve — twenty thousand
      Twenty thousand kings, each with his own retinue — the scale shows Jarāsandha's reach. Bhagavān's intervention, when it comes, will not be small either; the magnitude of bondage measures the magnitude of mercy needed.
    • prasahya ruddhāḥ — forcibly imprisoned
      Prasahya — by force. Saṁsāra too holds the jīva by force: the body, the mind, the karma-pāśa. The kings' imprisonment is a literalization of every jīva's predicament.
    Twenty thousand kings who had refused to submit absolutely to Jarāsandha during his world conquest had been forcibly imprisoned by him in the fortress named Girivraja.
    Purport

    Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī points out that these kings refused the payment of tribute and other forms of submission to Jarāsandha. Also, there is a well-known account in the Mahābhārata and other literatures that Jarāsandha desired to worship Mahā-bhairava by offering him the lives of one hundred thousand kings in sacrifice.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Twenty thousand kings who had refused to submit absolutely to Jarāsandha during his world conquest had been forcibly imprisoned by him in the fortress named Girivraja.
    Commentary

    How did Jarasandha imprison the kings? This verse answers. They did not accept subservience to him (sannatim) by paying taxes etc. Therefore he imprisoned them in Girivraja fort. How many? Twenty thousand (dve ayute) were imprisoned. It is learned from Mahabharata and other sources that he had the desire to perform a sacrifice to Mahabhairava by offering one hundred thousand kings.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The kings are described. They did not become very submissive (sannatim) to him and were thus imprisoned by Jarāsandha. The reason is described to the Lord to Yudhiṣṭhira: sa hi rājā jarāsandho yiyakṣur vasudhādhipaih Mahādevaṁ mahātmānam umāpatim arindama Jarāsandha wanted to perform a sacrifice to Śiva, using all the kings. Mahābhārata

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The kings, because they did not become submissive (sannatim) to Jarāsandha, had been imprisoned by him. Or “Who were the kings?” They were those who had did not submit. The reason is described by the Lord to Yudhiṣṭhira: sa hi rājā jarāsandho yiyakṣur vasudhādhipaih Mahādevaṁ mahātmānam umāpatim arindama Jarāsandha wanted to perform a sacrifice to Śiva, using all the kings. Mahābhārata

    Related values

    Related verses
    BG 5.7 BG 12.5 BG 5.4 BG 3.27 BG 10.4 BG 3.11 BG 7.8 BG 13.1 BG 12.6 BG 14.1
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Twenty thousand kings packed inside the fortress of Girivraja are the human race in saṁsāra in miniature — each a sovereign in his own mind, yet collectively a prisoner. Jarāsandha is the principle of bondage; Kṛṣṇa, the principle of release.

    Stories
    Jarāsandha's birth and curse

    In SB 9.22 and the Mahābhārata Sabhā-parva, Jarāsandha was born in two halves to two queens, joined together by the demoness Jarā — hence his name 'one who is joined by Jarā.' Cursed by Yavanarāja's lineage, he becomes Kṛṣṇa's sworn enemy and the kṣatriya-haṁsa-killer of his age.

    He represents bound-together-by-Jarā — the very name suggests how saṁsāra holds together by old-age and death. His twenty-thousand prisoners await release the way conditioned souls await mokṣa.

    📝 My note

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.70.25

    ↗
    రాజాన ఊచు: కృష్ణ కృష్ణాప్రమేయాత్మన్ ప్రపన్నభయభఞ్జన । వయం త్వాం శరణం యామో భవభీతా: పృథగ్ధియ: ॥ ౨౫ ॥

    Important Words
    • kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa — O Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa (doubled vocative)
      Reduplication in Sanskrit intensifies, but in nāma-bhakti the doubling is itself the rasa: the name once is calling, the name twice is taking shelter. Mahāprabhu's whole mahā-mantra is built on such reduplication.
    • aprameya-ātman — O immeasurable Soul
      Aprameya — beyond pramāṇa, beyond proof. The kings address Bhagavān as one whom no measure can contain, and yet they confidently call. Bhakti is the means by which the immeasurable becomes intimate.
    • prapanna-bhaya-bhañjana — destroyer of the fear of the surrendered
      A compound epithet that is one of the Bhāgavata's signature names for Bhagavān. The grammar is decisive: He destroys fear only for prapannas, those who have surrendered. Śaraṇāgati is the condition of His protection.
    • śaraṇam yāmaḥ — we come for shelter
      The verbatim phrase of śaraṇāgati. Echoes BG 18.66 (mām ekaṁ śaraṇaṁ vraja) and SB 7.5.23 (śravaṇaṁ kīrtanaṁ … ātma-nivedanam). The kings perform ātma-nivedana from inside their prison, and the rescue is already structurally ensured.
    • pṛthak-dhiyaḥ — of separated mentality
      They confess they are pṛthak-dhī — given to separate thinking, to the illusion of being independent agents. The confession is itself the beginning of bhakti: only one who admits pṛthak-dhī can give it up.
    The kings said [as related through their messenger]: O Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa, O immeasurable Soul, destroyer of fear for those surrendered to You! Despite our separatist attitude, we have come to You for shelter out of fear of material existence.
    Purport

    Śrīdhara Svāmī explains that the kings present their entreaty in this and the following five verses. In this verse they take shelter of the Lord, in the next three verses they describe their fear, and in the last two verses they make their prayerful request.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    The kings said [as related through their messenger]: O Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa, O immeasurable Soul, destroyer of fear for those surrendered to You ! Despite our separatist attitude, we have come to You for shelter out of fear of material existence.
    Commentary

    Six verses explain their request to the Lord. First they surrender by uttering twice "Krsna" out of respect. "Though we do not understand your true form (aprameyatman), we know that you are the protector of those who surrender to you (prapanna), and therefore we surrender to you. Neglecting a prayer for devotion to you, we instead are intent on deliverance from our own suffering, having intelligence separate from you, with our own interest (prthag dhiyah)."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The words of the kings are in six verses. O Svayam Bhagavān (kṛṣṇa)! Repetition of his name indicates their miserable condition. Because you are Svayam Bhagavān, you have unsurpassable powers (aprameyātman). Because you are supreme, we surrender to you since we fear saṁsāra. You can deliver us. Why are they afraid? Our intelligence is separated from you: we are devoid of bhakti.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The words of the kings are in six verses. O Svayam Bhagavān (kṛṣṇa)! Repetition of his name indicates their miserable condition. Because you are Svayam Bhagavān, you have unsurpassable powers (aprameyātman). Because you are supreme, we surrender to you, since we fear saṁsāra. You can deliver us. Why are we afraid? Our intelligence is separated from you. We see differences between friend and enemy, though actually jīvas have oneness. Or we have independent intelligence, though we are dependent on you, and are your aṁśas (pṛthadhiyaḥ). Or we think we are separate from you: we are devoid of bhakti.

    Related values

    Related lectures
    • NRM SB 10-70-25-47
      Her Grace Narayani Mataji· English
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    The kings cry 'kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa' — the very name repeated is the medicine and the message. Like a child calling 'mother, mother' in the dark, the doubled name is itself the saraṇāgati. They have not finished the sentence; the name has already begun the rescue.

    Stories
    Ajāmila calling Nārāyaṇa

    In SB 6.1-3, Ajāmila on his deathbed called out 'Nārāyaṇa' meaning only his son's name — yet the Viṣṇu-dūtas came, because the holy name has its own force, beyond the speaker's intention.

    The imprisoned kings here are wiser than Ajāmila — they actually intend Kṛṣṇa — but the principle is the same. Doubled name (kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa) is the original kīrtana, and the SB's whole nāma-doctrine sits in that little reduplication.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.70.26

    ↗
    లోకో వికర్మనిరత: కుశలే ప్రమత్త: కర్మణ్యయం త్వదుదితే భవదర్చనే స్వే । యస్తావదస్య బలవానిహ జీవితాశాం సద్యశ్ఛినత్త్యనిమిషాయ నమోఽస్తు తస్మై ॥ ౨౬ ॥
    loko vikarma-nirataḥ kuśale pramattaḥ
    [loko vikarma-niratah kushale pramattah]
    karmaṇy ayaṁ tvad-udite bhavad-arcane sve
    [karmany ayam tvad-udite bhavad-archane sve]

    [sadyash chinatty animishaaya namo ’stu tasmai]
    Word-for-word

    Important Words
    • animiṣāya — to the unblinking one (time)
      Animiṣa — without nimeṣa (blink). Bhagavān as time never blinks; nothing escapes His attention. The same epithet means both demigod (who do not blink) and Kṛṣṇa-as-kāla, in this verse the latter.
    • vikarma — forbidden activities
      Vi-karma is the technical opposite of dharma-karma — not just absence of duty but actively wrong action. The kings confess that the world is captive not only to the wicked Jarāsandha but to vikarma at large.
    • tvat-udite bhavat-arcane — in the worship of You as spoken by You
      The kings explicitly invoke the BG (cf. 18.66): worship as You yourself have commanded. Bhakti is not invented by the devotee; it is performed as Bhagavān Himself prescribed.
    • kuśale — what is for one's true benefit
      Kuśala — true welfare. The verse names sva-arcana (His own worship) as kuśala. All other 'goods' are pramāda — bewildered miscalculation. The Gauḍīya soteriology in one word.
    People in this world are always engaged in sinful activities and are thus bewildered about their real duty, which is to worship You according to Your commandments. This activity would truly bring them good fortune. Let us offer our obeisances unto the all-powerful Lord, who appears as time and suddenly cuts down one’s stubborn hope for a long life in this world.
    Purport

    Lord Kṛṣṇa states in the Bhagavad-gītā (9.27) : yat karoṣi yad aśnāsi yaj juhoṣi dadāsi yat yat tapasyasi kaunteya tat kuruṣva mad-arpaṇam “Whatever you do, whatever you eat, whatever you offer or give away, and whatever austerities you perform — do that, O son of Kuntī, as an offering to Me.” This is the Supreme Lord’s commandment, but people in general are bewildered and neglect this auspicious activity, preferring instead to perform sinful activities that lead them to terrible suffering. The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is working to enlighten the world about this most essential activity of loving service to the Lord.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    People in this world are always engaged in sinful activities and are thus bewildered about their real duty, which is to worship You according to Your commandments. This activity would truly bring them good fortune. Let us offer our obeisances unto the all-powerful Lord, who appears as time and suddenly cuts down one's stubborn hope for a long life in this world.
    Commentary

    In this verse they offer respects while describing their fear of material existence. Persons like us (loka) are inattentive to activities for our benefit (kusale karmani). To pious actions? No, we are inattentive to activities mentioned by you (tvad udite). Is it activity of sama , dama , yam and niyama in jnana yoga? No, we are inattentive to worship of you (bhavad arcane), which the real treasure (sve) of everyone. We are rather engaged in unlawful acts, which produce material happiness from wives and sons. Moreover such unfortunate souls never achieve any happiness, for you, being powerful, suddenly destroy our hopes for long life. We offer respects to you in the form of your energy of time. Those who are not your devotees should do this.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    “Why have you not worshipped me all this time? Why have you changed your minds now?” People like us are absorbed in sinful activities. That which is beneficial for us (kuśale) is that which gives all beneficial things and destroys all detrimental things. We have not been attentive to our dharma according to varṇa (sve karmaṇi) and have not been attentive to worshipping you, which is superior to other dharmas spoke by you and definitely superior to dharmas spoken by others. The Lord personally says: sarva-guhyatamaṁ bhūyaḥ śṛṇu me paramaṁ vacaḥ iṣṭo ’si me dṛḍham iti tato vakṣyāmi te hitam || Again hear from me the highest words, the supreme secret. Since you are very dear to me, I will speak for your benefit. man-manā bhava mad-bhakto mad-yājī māṁ namaskuru mām evaiṣyasi satyaṁ te pratijāne priyo ’si me ||65|| As a devotee, offer your mind to me. Offer articles to me in worship. Offer respects to me. I promise that you will come to me without doubt, for you are most dear to me. BG 64-65 It is not possible for us to give up this strong desire which blinds us to what is auspicious for us, and which engages us in opposite actions, making us reject surrender to you. You who are strong can cut this desire. We offer respects to he who is unblinking. He is unlike us who are inattentive (pramattaḥ), distracted by our strong desires. Because of fear, we are now eager to worship you. martyo mṛtyu-vyāla-bhītaḥ palāyan lokān sarvān nirbhayaṁ nādhyagacchat tvat pādābjaṁ prāpya yadṛcchayādya susthaḥ śete mṛtyur asmād apaiti No one in this material world, fearful of the snake of time, has become free from fear, even by fleeing to various planets. But now that you have appeared, death is fleeing in fear of you, and the living entities, having obtained shelter at your lotus feet by your mercy, are fearless and tranquil. SB 10.3.27

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    People are bewildered about activities which are beneficial (kuśale) for themselves (sve). They should necessarily to these actions for their own benefit. Not doing the actions is disobedience to the Lord and becomes a great offense, since these actions arise from you (tvad-udite). The action is easily performed, gives great results and gives the highest happiness. The action is related to deity forms of you and your devotees (bhavad-arcane). They are bewildered concerning this. The Lord cuts the strong desire to live, which instigates sinful acts, destroying thoughts of death, and bewilders people about what is actually beneficial for them. Or the Lord cuts the desire to live, by grave diseases, what to speak of destroying life itself. One cannot avoid the Lord. He is all powerful (balavān). And he is vigilant: he is never inattentive (animiṣāya). pramattam uccair iti kṛtya-cintayā pravṛddha-lobhaṁ viṣayeṣu lālasam tvam apramattaḥ sahasābhipadyase kṣul-lelihāno 'hir ivākhum antakaḥ With great attention, in the form of time, you suddenly seize the greedy person desiring material objects, who is intensely mad with thoughts of all the things he should do, just as a snake, licking its lips, seizes a mouse. SB 4.24.66 Or they offer respects to time (animiṣāya). Being afraid of time, since it is the main cause of material existence, they take shelter of the Lord.

      His Grace Revati Raman Prabhu· 2013-09-26· Telugu
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Bhagavān as time — animiṣa, the unblinking — cuts down the kings' hopes for long life as suddenly as a sword takes a flower's head. The verse offers obeisance to that very sword: bhakti consents to being trimmed, because the gardener is Kṛṣṇa.

    Stories
    Bhagavad-gītā 11.32

    Kṛṣṇa reveals to Arjuna: kālo 'smi loka-kṣaya-kṛt pravṛddho — 'I am time, the destroyer of worlds.' The same Lord whom the kings address as kāla here, hopeful that He will cut their stubborn illusions, is the cosmic time-form Arjuna saw.

    Gauḍīya doctrine: Kṛṣṇa's kāla-rūpa is mercy in another key. He cuts the false hope so that real hope — bhakti — may take its place.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.70.27

    ↗
    లోకే భవాఞ్జగదిన: కలయావతీర్ణ: సద్రక్షణాయ ఖలనిగ్రహణాయ చాన్య: । కశ్చిత్ త్వదీయమతియాతి నిదేశమీశ కిం వా జన: స్వకృతమృచ్ఛతి తన్న విద్మ: ॥ ౨౭ ॥

    Important Words
    • avatīrṇaḥ — descended
      Avatāra — the descent. The kings invoke the doctrine of avatāra at the precise moment when they need it most. The Gauḍīya understanding: every avatāra is for sat-rakṣaṇa and khala-nigraha, the verse's exact two phrases.
    • sat-rakṣaṇāya — for the protection of the saintly
      Sat — the truthful, the real, the devoted. Bhagavān's first descent-purpose is to protect them. The kings, naming themselves implicitly as sat, claim a place under that protection.
    • khala-nigrahaṇāya — for the subduing of the wicked
      Khala — the malicious. The second descent-purpose. The kings name Jarāsandha as khala, asking Bhagavān to fulfil the second clause of His own promise.
    • kalayā — by an expansion (Baladeva, or time potency)
      Bhagavān descends with His kalā — partial manifestation. The Gauḍīya reading: even when 'descending,' Bhagavān is in fact undiminished; only His outer manifestation appears partial.
    You are the predominating Lord of the universe and have descended into this world with Your personal power to protect the saintly and suppress the wicked. We cannot understand, O Lord, how anyone can transgress Your law and still continue to enjoy the fruits of his work.
    Purport

    Śrīdhara Svāmī explains that the kings were bewildered by the suffering that had come upon them. They state here that since the Lord has descended to this world to protect the pious and punish the wicked, how is it that Jarāsandha, who brazenly transgressed the order of the Lord, continued to perform his wicked activities, whereas the kings were put into a miserable condition? Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura similarly states that the kings could not understand how Jarāsandha, who harassed the saintly devotees and nourished the envious, could continue to prosper, whereas the kings were being tormented by the wicked Jarāsandha. Similarly Śrīla Prabhupāda quotes the kings as follows in Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead: “My dear Lord, You are the proprietor of all the worlds, and You have incarnated Yourself along with Your plenary expansion Lord Balarāma. It is said that Your appearance in this incarnation is for the purpose of protecting the faithful and destroying the miscreants. Under the circumstances, how is it possible that miscreants like Jarāsandha can put us into such deplorable conditions of life against Your authority? We are puzzled at the situation and cannot understand how it is possible. It may be that Jarāsandha has been deputed to give us such trouble because of our past misdeeds, but we have heard from revealed scriptures that anyone who surrenders unto Your lotus feet immediately becomes immune to the reactions of sinful life… [We] therefore… wholeheartedly offer ourselves unto Your shelter, and we hope that Your Lordship will now give us full protection.”

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    You are the predominating Lord of the universe and have descended into this world with Your personal power to protect the saintly and suppress the wicked. We cannot understand, O Lord, how anyone can transgress Your law and still continue to enjoy the fruits of his work.
    Commentary

    It is greatly astonishing to us why anyone who hates you or your devotees is not destroyed. The lord of the universe (jagad inah) has appeared at a certain time (kalaya) or with his portion Baladeva, for protecting the pious and destroying the evil. However, other wicked men, such as Jarasandha, surpass your law and persecute the devotees and protect the wicked. Though you appear to destroy the evil men, are they prospering and happy due to past pious acts? Though you appear to protect the devotees, are they suffering now due to their past sins? It is not possible for us to understand this. It should not be that dull material karma should be more powerful than you.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    You are directly the Lord of the universe (jagad-inaḥ) and have appeared with your expansion Baladeva in this world. By what cause can others, the wicked who have not been suppressed, disregard your laws? O Lord, even Jarāsandha is under your control. He is under the control of karma. We do not know how anyone can surpass your law.

    Related values & verses
    Related values

    Concepts
    Karma
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Bhagavān descends to protect the saintly and subdue the wicked — the kings cannot understand how Jarāsandha still rampages. It is as if the sun has risen and yet shadows persist; the kings rightly suspect that the shadow is not stronger than the sun but is being permitted, for some purpose, to remain.

    Stories
    Bhagavad-gītā 4.7-8

    Kṛṣṇa's own classic statement: yadā yadā hi dharmasya glānir bhavati … paritrāṇāya sādhūnāṁ vināśāya ca duṣkṛtām. The verse the kings paraphrase here is from BG 4.7-8, where Bhagavān states the law of His descent.

    The kings effectively quote Him back to Himself — a beautiful bhakti move. They ask Him to act on His own self-stated promise. The Gauḍīya commentators note: this is the proper way to pray, by reminding Bhagavān of His own scripture.

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.70.28

    ↗
    స్వప్నాయితం నృపసుఖం పరతన్త్రమీశ శశ్వద్భ‍యేన మృతకేన ధురం వహామ: । హిత్వా తదాత్మని సుఖం త్వదనీహలభ్యం క్లిశ్యామహేఽతికృపణాస్తవ మాయయేహ ॥ ౨౮ ॥
    svapnāyitaṁ nṛpa-sukhaṁ para-tantram īśa

    Important Words
    • svapnāyitam — merely dreamlike
      Svapnāyita — like a dream. The technical Bhāgavata term for saṁsāra-sukha: it has the appearance of substance but no abiding reality. The kings finally see what they could not see before imprisonment.
    • para-tantram — dependent (on something else)
      Their happiness is para-tantra — under another's control (specifically Jarāsandha's, but more deeply, māyā's). True happiness is sva-tantra, which is only in bhakti to Bhagavān.
    • tvat-anīha labhyam — to be obtained by selfless work done for You
      Anīha — without selfish craving. Niṣkāma-karma-yoga (BG 2.47-48, 3.9) is the Gauḍīya doorway: work performed for Bhagavān, without personal claim, becomes the road to ātmani sukham — happiness within the self.
    • kṛpaṇāḥ — wretched, miserly
      Kṛpaṇa is the SB's term (cf. 7.6.10) for one who fails to use the rare human birth for bhakti. The kings confess themselves as kṛpaṇa — the first step in becoming udāra (generous-hearted, devoted).
    • māyayā — by the illusory energy
      Bhagavān's own deluding potency. The kings recognize that their suffering is not random — it is His māyā operating on them, and so the remedy is Him. The metaphysics points the way out.
    O Lord, with this corpselike body, always full of fear, we bear the burden of the relative happiness of kings, which is just like a dream. Thus we have rejected the real happiness of the soul, which comes by rendering selfless service to You. Being so very wretched, we simply suffer in this life under the spell of Your illusory energy.
    Purport

    After expressing their doubts in the previous verse, the kings herein admit that actually they are suffering because of their own foolishness, having given up the eternal happiness of the soul in exchange for the temporary, conditional happiness of a so-called kingly position. Most people make a similar mistake, desiring wealth, power, prestige, aristocratic family and so on, in exchange for their own soul. The kings admit that they have fallen under the spell of the Lord’s illusory energy and have mistaken the tremendous anxiety of political leadership for happiness.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    O Lord, with this corpselike body, always full of fear, we bear the burden of the relative happiness of kings, which is just like a dream. Thus we have rejected the real happiness of the soul, which comes by rendering selfless service to You. Being so very wretched, we simply suffer in this life under the spell of Your illusory energy.
    Commentary

    "Then are you my devotees or my enemies?" "We are not either. We are jivas engulfed in the material world who have surrendered to you." That is the import of this verse. Our happiness, thinking ourselves kings, dependent on ministers, friends and troops, is temporary like a dream. Actually, we are carrying a great burden from intense endeavors at alliance and war, with our corpse like bodies filled with constant fear. It is unfortunate that we did not previously become desireless and take shelter of you. We gave up that happiness which is famous because of the praises of the saintly persons, not condemned by them like our kingly happiness, which is yourself, not dependent on others, coming from you alone, not from gross material objects, obtained by your selfless devotees (aniha labhya), not by those full of lust. Therefore we suffer.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Even today we have not completely given up māyā. Having lost our intelligence (ati-kṛpaṇāḥ) by your māyā, we suffer, because we carry the burden of this body which is like an unconscious corpse. Not only do we suffer by carrying this corpse, but we suffer by constant fear of sickness and time, which are like ghosts and spirits haunting us. “Why do you carry this burden?” It gives happiness of a king. We do not reject that burden even now because we want to attain the position of a king. This is to be condemned. And this happiness is not real since it is like a dream, not substantial. And it is filled with misery. Having a kingdom is dependent on many factors. That would be alright if there was no other great happiness that could be attained. We have given up the highest happiness which is testified in all the scriptures. This happiness is not dependent on other things since it manifests on its own within the ātmā. It can be attained from you by your devotees.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Our happiness is like a dream, temporary and illusory. It is without happiness. The body by which we try to enjoy ends up giving suffering with constant sickness. We carry our burden with constant fear, from sickness etc. (śaśvat bhayena), ascertaining death, detestable like death. In either case there is suffering in living. O Lord! We belong to you. Giving up the happiness which is attained by your mercy, the happiness of realizing you in truth, or meditating on you, we suffer in saṁsāra (iha). We experience plenty of suffering because we have become most foolish (ati-kṛpaṇāḥ) by your insurmountable māyā. Or we carry the burden of happiness of enjoying as kings. We have given up the indescribable happiness in the heart or our svarūpa, derived from your lotus feet and suffer in this world, which is ornamented by your appearance. Or not only from Jarāsandha, but by your māyā we always suffering. Or do we make Jarāsandha suffer through our suffering? We always suffer because of our faults.

      His Holiness Ramai Swami· 2007-11-10· English
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    The kings carry their royal happiness like men hauling a corpse — the body of pleasure has long died, and yet they cannot put it down. Bhakti is the only knife sharp enough to cut the rope, and they finally name themselves as kṛpaṇa — wretchedly attached.

    Stories
    Bharata Mahārāja and the deer

    In SB 5.7-14, Bharata Mahārāja, a great king and yogī, became so attached to a baby deer that he forgot his sādhana and was reborn as a deer himself. Even royal happiness, even sannyāsa, can be undone by misplaced affection.

    The imprisoned kings here see the same trap in themselves: nṛpa-sukham — kingly happiness — is svapnāyita (dreamlike), and they have been carrying that dream-corpse for lifetimes. Bharata's story is the warning the verse implicitly cites.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    sheep .
    Important Words
    • praṇata-śoka-hara — remover of the sorrow of the surrendered
      Triple compound: praṇata (the bowed-down), śoka (grief), hara (remover). One of Bhagavān's most precise epithets — He removes the sorrow specifically of those who bow. Bowing is the door.
    • karma-pāśāt — from the fetters of karma
      Karma-pāśa — the rope of karma. The kings' physical chains are doubled by metaphysical chains, and both are loosened together. Bhagavān's grace cuts both kinds of bondage at once.
    • mṛga-rāṭ — lion (king of beasts)
      The lion-image is doubled in the chapter: Jarāsandha is the false lion that has caged the kings; Kṛṣṇa among the Yadus was just called nr-siṁha (18). The true Lion will displace the false one.
    • viyuṅkṣva — please separate (us from the bondage)
      From vi + yuj — to unjoin, to release. The verbal imperative carries the entire request: separate us from our fetters. Bhakti often takes the form of begging Bhagavān to unjoin what should not be joined.
    Therefore, since Your feet relieve the sorrow of those who surrender to them, please release us prisoners from the shackles of karma, manifest as the King of Magadha. Wielding alone the prowess of ten thousand maddened elephants, he has locked us up in his house just as a lion captures sheep.
    Purport

    The kings here pray for the Lord to release them from the bondage of karma created by the Lord’s material potency. The kings make it clear that Jarāsandha is so powerful that there is no hope for them to escape by their own power.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Therefore, since Your feet relieve the sorrow of those who surrender to them, please release us prisoners from the shackles of karma, manifest as the King of Magadha. Wielding alone the prowess of ten thousand maddened elephants, he has locked us up in his house just as a lion captures sheep.
    Commentary

    In this verse they pray that Krsna release them from the bondage of karma created by his maya. "Please release (viyuksva) us from the bondage of karma in the form of Jarasandha." "But you can get out of this bondage of Jarasandha by yourselves." "Having the power of a hundred thousand elephants he alone has imprisoned all of us kings, as lion catches sheep."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Since we foolish persons are not free of desires, please free us from this bondage, an obstacle to having you manifest yourself, so that we can achieve the happiness in the ātmā. Seeing that we have lost our intelligence because of your māyā (tat), please free us from prison, since your feet can destroy suffering of those who surrender to you. Bhavān can also be taken as abhavān: those who do not have auspiciousness, who are suffering greatly. Free us from the bondage of karma in the form of Jarāsandha. If it were not for our karma, he would not have the power to lock us up.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Because of suffering caused by māyā (tat), free us from the chains of karma, because your feet destroy the suffering of surrendered persons. Or free us who have no auspiciousness (abhavān), who are suffering intensely from the ropes of our karma personified as Jarāsandha. He is only the pretext for our karmas. Or he could not have such power unless karma was involved.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Jarāsandha caging twenty thousand kings as a lion captures sheep — the image is exact: those who lived as kings have been reduced to flock-animals. Only the king of kings, Kṛṣṇa, can break the lion's grip, because He is the lion-master Himself (Nṛsiṁha).

    Stories
    Bhīma killing Jarāsandha

    In SB 10.71-72, Kṛṣṇa will bring Bhīma to Girivraja, and Bhīma — wrestling for fourteen days — will kill Jarāsandha by tearing him in two along the original join. The kings here, praying for release, do not yet know the means Kṛṣṇa will use; they only know Him.

    The Gauḍīya reading: bhakti's prayer does not stipulate the method, only the deliverer. Bhagavān then chooses His instruments — here, Bhīma's strength — to fulfil what the prayer requested.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.70.30

    ↗
    యో వై త్వయా ద్వినవకృత్వ ఉదాత్తచక్ర భగ్నో మృధే ఖలు భవన్తమనన్తవీర్యమ్ । జిత్వా నృలోకనిరతం సకృదూఢదర్పో యుష్మత్ప్రజా రుజతి నోఽజిత తద్ విధేహి ॥ ౩౦ ॥

    Important Words
    • dvi-nava-kṛtvaḥ — eighteen times (two-times-nine)
      Bhāgavata commentators traditionally read this as seventeen victories and one strategic 'defeat' — the eighteenth as the Mucukunda-trick. The number-grammar carries the entire history.
    • nr-loka-niratam — absorbed in human affairs
      Bhagavān 'absorbed in human affairs' — a doctrinally weighty phrase. He chooses to act as if bound by human limits, hiding His full sovereignty. Gauḍīya: this is His narottama-līlā, His human-pastime; never a real diminution.
    • ananta-vīryam — of unlimited prowess
      Ananta — endless. The kings explicitly affirm Bhagavān's unbounded strength even while reporting His apparent loss. Faith outruns appearance — the basic bhakti operation.
    • ajita — O unconquerable one
      Ajita — never-conquered. Kṛṣṇa's epithet, used precisely when He has 'allowed' Himself to be conquered. The contradiction is theological: He is ajita in essence even when līlā shows otherwise.
    O wielder of the disc! Your strength is unlimited, and thus seventeen times You crushed Jarāsandha in battle. But then, absorbed in human affairs, You allowed him to defeat You once. Now he is so filled with pride that he dares to torment us, Your subjects. O unconquerable one, please rectify this situation.
    Purport

    The word nṛ-loka-niratam indicates that the Lord was absorbed in playing within the world of human beings. Thus, while acting like a human king He allowed Jarāsandha to be victorious in a single battle after the Lord had crushed him seventeen times. The kings here imply that Jarāsandha is especially harassing them because they are souls surrendered to Lord Kṛṣṇa. Therefore they beg the Lord, “O You who hold the cakra weapon high, please make the proper arrangement.” Śrīla Prabhupāda expresses the kings’ feelings as follows: “My dear Lord, You have already fought with Jarāsandha eighteen times consecutively, out of which You have defeated him seventeen times by surpassing his extraordinary powerful position. But in Your eighteenth fight You exhibited Your human behavior, and thus it appeared that You were defeated. My dear Lord, we know very well that Jarāsandha cannot defeat You at any time, because Your power, strength, resources and authority are all unlimited. No one can equal You or surpass You. The appearance of defeat by Jarāsandha in the eighteenth engagement is nothing but an exhibition of human behavior. Unfortunately, foolish Jarāsandha could not understand Your tricks, and he has since then become puffed up over his material power and prestige. Specifically, he has arrested us and imprisoned us, knowing fully that as Your devotees, we are subordinate to Your sovereignty.”

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    O wielder of the disc! Your strength is unlimited, and thus seventeen times You crushed Jarāsandha in battle. But then, absorbed in human affairs, You allowed him to defeat You once. Now he is so filled with pride that he dares to torment us, Your subjects. O unconquerable one, please rectify this situation.
    Commentary

    Your enemy, knowing that we have surrendered to you, now persecutes us even more day by day. That is expressed in this verse. "O you who hold the cakra high, in the eighteen times he fought with you, he was defeated seventeen times. Only once defeating you, who desire to take up the pastimes of fleeing away like an ordinary man, he swelled with pride, and persecuted us, your subjects. Do what is proper in this case (tat)."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Just by holding up your cakra you can defeat him! Absorbed in your human pastimes, you allowed him to defeat you once.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Just by holding up your cakra you can defeat him! It is well known (vai) that even though (khalu) he was defeated by you seventeen times, one time he defeated you, who have unlimited power and play a human role Or he defeated you once, though (khalu) you have unlimited power. Or he defeated you once, though you know that you should protect your citizens (khalu). The plural is used (yuṣmat) to indicate that the citizens belong to Balarāma and others as well as Kṛṣṇa. Or they indicate that all the devotees should protect them, out of extreme misery. O unconquered one, even now you have not been actually defeated by Jarāsandh. He defeated you once, of unlimited power, though you teach humans how to act (nṛloka-niratam) and should never flee from other warriors. Or he defeated you once, since you are absorbed in human behavior. Jarāsandha became very proud. Please do something so that he does not torment us.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Kṛṣṇa has defeated Jarāsandha seventeen times and once allowed Himself to lose — like a teacher who scores his pupil's pride and then deliberately fails an exam to let the pupil swell. The kings, having watched the swelling become a torment, now ask Him to end the game.

    Stories
    Mucukunda and the burning of Kālayavana

    In SB 10.50-51, Kṛṣṇa's eighteenth encounter with Jarāsandha: He pretended to flee, leading Kālayavana to the cave of the sleeping king Mucukunda, who incinerated Kālayavana with a glance. Kṛṣṇa's apparent 'defeat' was the staged setting of a deeper victory.

    The kings here do not yet know that the 'defeat' was lila. They only see Jarāsandha's inflated pride. Bhagavān's losses are always strategic; bhakti trusts the strategy even when it is opaque.

    Important Words
    • prapannāḥ — surrendered
      Prapanna — the technical term for one who has performed śaraṇāgati. The messenger does not say 'powerful kings'; he says 'surrendered.' Their qualification before Bhagavān is no longer their crown but their prapatti.
    • darśana-kāṅkṣiṇaḥ — anxiously desiring darśana
      Darśana — seeing — is the Vaiṣṇava goal even more than physical release. The kings ask first to see Him; freedom is secondary. Bhakti subordinates mokṣa to darśana.
    • śam vidhīyatām — please bestow welfare
      Śam — auspiciousness, peace. The imperative is gentle: the kings do not specify what they want; they entrust the form of help to Him. Bhakti is request without dictation.
    • dīnānām — of the wretched, of the lowly
      Dīna — humbled, destitute. Bhagavān is dīna-bandhu (friend of the wretched) and dīna-nātha. The kings, having become dīna, qualify for the very name that Bhagavān bears toward them.
    The messenger continued: This is the message of the kings imprisoned by Jarāsandha, who all hanker for Your audience, having surrendered to Your feet. Please bestow good fortune on these poor souls.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    The messenger continued: This is the message of the kings imprisoned by Jarāsandha, who all hanker for Your audience, having surrendered to Your feet. Please bestow good fortune on these poor souls.
    Commentary

    In this verse the messenger speaks.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    After revealing their suffering through their message, the messenger concluded. The kings are suffering greatly and are surrendered to you. Because they are imprisoned in Girivraja, like children, they hanker to see you. Not only should you free them. You should also show yourself to them. Therefore you should bestow good fortune on these suffering kings at this time.

    Analogy

    The messenger ends his speech the way an arrow lodges in its mark — 'they hanker for Your audience, bestow good fortune.' Like a child holding up a broken toy to a parent, his report is complete the moment Bhagavān has heard it; the rest is His to do.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.70.32

    ↗
    శ్రీశుక ఉవాచ రాజదూతే బ్రువత్యేవం దేవర్షి: పరమద్యుతి: । బిభ్రత్పిఙ్గజటాభారం ప్రాదురాసీద్ యథా రవి: ॥ ౩౨ ॥

    Important Words
    • deva-ṛṣiḥ — the sage of the demigods
      Devarṣi — Nārada's specific title. He is the only ṛṣi who freely moves among devas, asuras, and mortals, carrying news of Bhagavān. The Gauḍīya tradition reveres him as the guru of Vyāsa and thus the great-grandfather of the SB itself.
    • piṅga-jaṭā — yellowish matted hair
      Piṅga — reddish-yellow, the colour of fire and sun. Nārada wears no royal ornament but his hair itself shines like the sun. The ascetic-luminous body is a Vaiṣṇava aesthetic: holiness as its own radiance.
    • yathā raviḥ — like the sun
      Nārada is compared to the sun just as Kṛṣṇa was at verse 15 (bhāskaraḥ). Two suns now share the hall. The simile encodes Gauḍīya doctrine: the devotee shines with Bhagavān's borrowed-and-yet-real light.
    • prādurāsīt — appeared, manifested
      Prādur-bhū is the verb of divine manifestation (cf. avirbhāva). Nārada does not 'enter' through a door so much as 'manifest' in the court — his arrivals are always slightly miraculous.
    Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: When the kings’ messenger had thus spoken, the sage of the demigods, Nārada, suddenly appeared. Bearing a mass of golden matted locks on his head, the supremely effulgent sage entered like the brilliant sun.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: When the kings' messenger had thus spoken, the sage of the demigods, Nārada, suddenly appeared. Bearing a mass of golden matted locks on his head, the supremely effulgent sage entered like the brilliant sun.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    At this time he was manifesting more effulgence that ever (parama-dyutiḥ). This was arranged by the Lord so that people would listen to his words.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    When the messenger of the kings was speaking in this way (evam), and the Lord did not say anything in response, Nārada appeared, so that the Lord would first go to Yudhiṣṭhira’s house. Nārada was shining or had great power and had golden hair. He was like the sun. In this way he appeared in order that, by showing his nature, his words would be accepted. Or he was joyful that his desired rājasūya sacrifice would be performed when the Lord came to Yudhiṣṭhira’s house. Or Śukadeva describes him in this way because he is filled with bhakti for him since he came there on behalf of Yudhiṣṭhira. Or Nārada was radiant with great power of austerity (parama-dyutiḥ). This indicates his omniscience. Knowing the Lord would go to Yudhiṣṭhira’s house, he came here to hasten the event. The power of his austerity is indicated by his shining hair. He was like the sun: most powerful or most worthy of worship.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Nārada entering with golden matted locks blazing like the sun is like one luminous body announcing another. The court that already had its sun (Kṛṣṇa) now sees a second sun walk in — and rises to greet him, because the second sun is His servant.

    Stories
    Nārada's previous birth

    In SB 1.5-6, Nārada relates how in a previous birth he was the son of a maidservant who served sages; their leftover food and conversation purified him so completely that in his next birth he became the eternal sage Nārada, traveling at will, singing of Bhagavān.

    His appearance at the Dvārakā court here is no surprise to him or to Kṛṣṇa — he is the universal messenger who carries kṛṣṇa-kathā between the worlds. The verse situates him as the link between Pāṇḍava-loka and Kṛṣṇa's court.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.70.33

    ↗
    తం ద‍ృష్ట్వా భగవాన్ కృష్ణ: సర్వలోకేశ్వరేశ్వర: । వవన్ద ఉత్థిత: శీర్ష్ణా ససభ్య: సానుగో ముదా ॥ ౩౩ ॥
    taṁ dṛṣṭvā bhagavān kṛṣṇaḥ
